<<<PAGE 10>>>
WIFI EXPRESS – ACCESS GROUP
An AP-Group consists of 
several APs connected via the 
LAN in "overlay" mode and 
without infrastructure 
modification
The group is identified by an 
"group ID" and all APs that have or 
will have the same ID are put 
under management of the PVM.
Another AP is responsible for rescuing the 
Primary Virtual Manager (PVM). It is called 
Secondary Virtual Manager (SVM)
In an AP group, one AP supports the 
role of centralized management. It is 
called PVM (Primary Virtual Manager)
All other APs are under 
management of the PVM of the 
group. They are called Members
PVM
Primary Virtual 
Manager
SVM
Secondary Virtual 
Manager

<<<PAGE 11>>>
WIFI EXPRESS – ACCESS GROUP PVM ELECTION
AP with the second highest MAC is 
designated as the SVM
Once the PVM is designated, it 
sends an SSID for the 
configuration of the AP-group
mywifi-0102 
All other APs become members 
of the group with up to 255 APs 
in a group.
In the case of a VLAN with several APs 
started at the same time an election 
process is perform to select the PVM
Highest Model Type
Highest MAC address

MODE SELECTION
• WiFi Express is the default mode
• AP requests and receives an IP address from the DHCP server.
• DHCP option 138 equals the IP address of the OmniVista 2500 Server
WiFi Express
WiFi Enterprise
subnet 192.168.10.0 netmask 255.255.255.0
{
dynamic-dhcp range 192.168.10.10 192.168.10.20
{
option subnet-mask 255.255.255.0;
option broadcast-address 192.168.10.255;
option routers 192.168.10.1;
option dhcp-lease-time 6000;
option domain-name-servers 192.168.10.1;
option domain-name "vlan10.home";
option 138 192.168.0.61;
}
}
subnet 192.168.10.0 netmask 255.255.255.0
{
dynamic-dhcp range 192.168.10.10 192.168.10.20
{
option subnet-mask 255.255.255.0;
option broadcast-address 192.168.10.255;
option routers 192.168.10.1;
option dhcp-lease-time 6000;
option domain-name-servers 192.168.10.1;
option domain-name "vlan10.home";
}
}

<<<PAGE 18>>>
CHANGING THE MODE
• Mode can be changed:
• Manually in Express mode with a "Convert to Enterprise" button
• Or requires a factory reset (push button) and reboot
• Migrate an existing Cluster (WiFi Express) to OV mode (WiFi Enterprise)
• Add option 138 in the DHCP server for the AP management scope
• Perform a factory reset/reboot or change the mode manually
dhcpd.conf
Option 138
No configuration migration, AP « cluster » configuration is lost

• Type of Traffic

<<<PAGE 24>>>
DATA PLANE
• Type of traffic
• Forward data user traffic 
• Manages the QoS and ACLs
• Data Plane Traffic 
• Wireless data converted to Ethernet in the AP 
and sent to the AP uplink
• Wireless traffic always tagged on the AP uplink
• No tunnel mode to OV or Virtual Controller
• Data Plane is only L2
• No routing for data user traffic
• Routing provided by LAN infrastructure

<<<PAGE 31>>>
BLE BEACONING
• BLE Beaconing ready for the AP1230, AP13XX series with a built-in BLE
• Stellar APs ready for Asset Tracking Solution
• Asset: people or equipment (wheel chair, medical devices, laptop,…)
• Reducing time to find assets: improves employees/customer satisfaction
• BLE Beacon is configured per AP Group
• Turned OFF by default
• Configurable parameters are
• Beaconing Mode : iBeacon per default
• Transmission Power
• Frequency/Emission Period
• UUID (Universal Unique Identifier) – ALE specific UUID for all ALE products
• Major and Minor values – used for greater accuracy than UUID alone
OAW-AP1230 Series

<<<PAGE 32>>>
INTEGRATION WITH AEROSCOUT LOCATION ENGINE
• AeroScout RTLS (Real Time Location Services) provides location services.
• i.g: Tracking of employees in the building at the plant
• AeroScout solution utilize standard WiFi (802.11) technologies as 
a communication infrastructure
• Customers use the Stellar AP to communicate with AeroScout tags 
and deliver information to the AeroScout Location Engine
• AeroScout LBS Architecture
• AeroScout Tags: Device generating 802.11 messages at a predefined interval
• Stellar APs: Delivers RSSI measurements of tags and WiFi clients 
to the AeroScout Engine
• AeroScout Engine Server (AES): Location Engine. Based on RSSI measurements 
(from the Stellar AP), determine position of the clients
• AeroScout Engine Manager (AEM): Configuration of the AES. 
Displays clients on the map, heatmaps, analytics, Geofencing alerts
Stellar AP
AeroScout tags

<<<PAGE 33>>>
EXAMPLE CONFIGURATION (ISC-DHCP-SERVER)
• Linux open source DHCP server
• OmniSwitch used as DHCP server
• Dhcpd.conf file configuration:
# Classify OmniAccess Stellar AP as STELLAR
class "STELLAR" {
match if substring (option vendor-class-
identifier, 0, 4) = "HAP.";
}
# Create custom option 138 as it is not known 
to isc-dhcp-server
option ovwma code 138 = ip-address;
DHCP Pool
subnet 192.168.10.0 netmask 255.255.255.0 {
…
# Pool for OmniAccess Stellar AP
pool {
allow members of "STELLAR";
range 192.168.10.10 192.168.10.20;
option ovwma 192.168.0.61;
}
}
subnet 192.168.10.0 netmask 255.255.255.0
{
dynamic-dhcp range 192.168.10.10 192.168.10.20
{
option subnet-mask 255.255.255.0;
option broadcast-address 192.168.10.255;
option routers 192.168.10.1;
option dhcp-lease-time 6000;
option domain-name-servers 192.168.10.1;
option domain-name "vlan10.home";
option 138 192.168.0.61;

<<<PAGE 71>>>
3 
Lab: Connection & Use of the Stellar Remote Lab 
 
 2 
Discover the Remote Lab Topology 
In this part, you will find an explanation about the Remote Lab topology that you are going to use during this 
training.  
2.1. 
Topology of the Stellan Remote Lab Pod 
 
 
This topology contains: clients, Stellar Access points, OmniSwitches, servers and an OmniVista Cirrus. 
It simulates a three-tier end user topology (Access, Aggregation and Core), as well as the clients, servers and 
internet connection to your OmniVista Cirrus. 
2.2. 
Stellar Access Points 
You will manage and configure the Stellar Access Points AP1301 and AP1321 through the OmniVista Cirrus. 
You will configure the network, so they can communicate with the DHCP server and send traffic to the 
OmniVista Cirrus through the internet link. 
2.3. 
Access Layer and Clients 
 
You will manage and configure the wired (Client 05) and wireless client (Raspberry Pi “Wifi client”). 
You will manage and configure both Access OmniSwitches (OS-6360 and OS-2360). 
All the traffic will be switched (L2 switching) at this level. And DHCP, DNS, HTTP,… requests will be 
forwarded to the aggregation switch, then to the servers in our core network.

<<<PAGE 72>>>
4 
Lab: Connection & Use of the Stellar Remote Lab 
 
2.4. 
Aggregation Layer 
 
You will manage and configure parts of the aggregation OmniSwitch OS6870. 
But, DO NOT MODIFY OR DELETE the default configuration loaded on the OS-6870 by the reset script 
(explained in the next lab). 
All the layer 3 routing protocols will be preconfigured by the script. And some Layer 2 VLANs can be 
preconfigured. 
Keep this default configuration and build on top of it as explained during the labs of this training. 
The OS6870 implements features such as DHCP relay and dynamic routing. This allows your Clients and Stellar 
Access Points to communicate with the servers and OmniVista Cirrus. 
2.5. 
Core Layer 
 
DO NOT MANAGE AND CONFIGURE the core switch OS6900. Its job, with his default configuration pushed by 
our reset script, is to route (OSPF, RIP) the traffic between our three OmniSwitches and our servers. 
This OS6900 will be transparent during your training, this is why you don’t have a shortcut to reach it. 
2.6. 
Servers 
 
You must have the DHCP server (“AAA Training Server POD##”) and NAT/Firewall server (“Pod##_pfSense”) 
started and running to perform the labs. 
But, DO NOT MANAGE AND CONFIGURE these servers. They are preconfigured with all the scopes, routes,.. 
needed. 
The Stellar Access Points and all the clients who will be authenticated will get their IP address from this 
DHCP server. 
And all the outgoing traffic (to OmniVista Cirrus and internet) have to go through the NAT and Firewall VM.

OMNIACCESS STELLAR AP GROUPS – ROLES

PVM

in the AP Group, another Stellar AP is elected SVM

Members

•
Criteria 1 : highest Stellar AP model
•
Criteria 2 : highest MAC address

Devices Startup & Connection 
 
 1 
Introduction 
In this lab, we are going to learn how to connect to the administration interfaces of the OmniSwitch 6360 and 
the OmniAccess Stellar 1321 access point.  
 
 
Note 
The OmniAccess Stellar AP1321 access point doesn’t need dedicated power cables: the OmniSwitch 6360 
provides power. This feature, called PoE or Power over Ethernet, will be viewed in detail later during this 
training.

<<<PAGE 110>>>
3 
Devices Startup & Connection 
 
1.1. 
First Connection: OmniSwitch 6360 Console Port 
By default, the OmniSwitch 6360 can only be configured from the console port. For security reasons, the 
other protocols (Telnet, SSH, Web Interface) are disabled.  
 
 
Connect to the OmniSwitch 6360 from the console port with the default credentials 
 
Use the SW5-OS-6360 shortcut to connect to the OmniSwitch 6360 from the console port: 
 
Remote Desktop 
Double click on SW5-
OS-6360A 
 
Press Enter  
 
Fill the credentials:  
- login: admin 
- password: switch 
 
 2 
Modifying the OmniSwitch 6360 Configuration 
2.1. 
Enabling the Stellar AP1321 port on the OmniSwitch SW5-6360A  
- In the default configuration loaded on the OmniSwitch 6360, the user ports are disabled. First, enable 
the port 1/1/6 (where is connected the Stellar AP1321): 
 
OS6360-XTE210 -> interfaces 1/1/6 admin-state enable 
2.2. 
Enabling the LAN Client VM port on the OmniSwitch SW5-6360A  
- Enable the port 1/1/1 (where is connected the LAN Client VM Client5): 
 
OS6360-XTE210 -> interfaces 1/1/1 admin-state enable 
2.3. 
Configuring the OmniSwitch 6360 Management IP address  
 
 
Configure the OmniSwitch 6360 IP interface > 192.168.1.2  
 
- Create an IP interface on the OmniSwitch 6360 to reach the AP Stellar 1321: 
 
OS6360-XTE210 -> ip interface int_1 address 192.168.1.2/24 vlan 1 
2.3.1. 
Verification 
- To check that the IP interface has been created, use the following command:

<<<PAGE 111>>>
4 
Devices Startup & Connection 
 
 
-> show ip interface 
Total 2 interfaces 
 Flags (D=Directly-bound) 
 
            Name                 IP Address      Subnet Mask     Status Forward  Device   Flags 
--------------------------------+---------------+---------------+------+-------+---------+------ 
EMP-CHAS1                        10.4.32.5       255.255.255.0   UP     NO      EMP 
EMP-CMMA-CHAS1                   0.0.0.0         0.0.0.0         DOWN   NO      EMP 
Loopback                         127.0.0.1       255.255.255.255     UP      NO Loopback 
int_1                            192.168.1.2     255.255.255.0       UP     YES vlan 1 
 
- The IP interface “Loopback” is the internal OmniSwitch 6360 IP address and cannot be used as 
Management IP address. 
2.4. 
Save the configuration 
 
Save the configuration 
 
- To save the switch configuration:  
 
-> write memory flash-synchro

<<<PAGE 112>>>
5 
Devices Startup & Connection 
 
 3 
Connecting to the OmniAccess Stellar AP1321 Access Point 
3.1. 
Before Beginning 
 
 
 
- The OmniAccess Stellar AP1321 is connected to the OmniSwitch 6360 on the port 1/1/6. 
- In this lab, we will not use any DHCP server. The AP’s IP address that we will use is the default one 
(192.168.1.254). 
- In this exercise, we will connect to the access point administration interface from the wired client VM 
(Wired Client5). We could also have connected to it via the default SSID broadcasted by the access point. 
We will study in detail the default configuration of an OmniAccess Stellar access point later in this 
training. 
3.2. 
Connecting to the Web Interface 
 
- Access the AP Stellar 1321 administration web interface  
- Login with the default credentials 
 
- Connect to the WiredClient Client 5 VM to configure the Stellar AP1321.  
- Modify the VM IP address to 192.168.1.200:

Devices Startup & Connection 
 
6.2. 
Reinitializing the OmniSwitch 6360 
 
Warning 
DON’T TEST THE FOLLOWING PART ON YOUR LAB! THE SWITCHES THAT ARE USED IN OUR REMOTE-LAB ARE 
LOADED WITH A SPECIFIC DEFAULT CONFIGURATION. REINITIALIZING THEM TO THEIR FACTORY DEFAULT 
CONFIGURATION MAY LEAD TO ISSUES!  
 
OS6360 
Wait for the 
OmniSwitch to boot 
up completely 
(approx. 3 minutes) 
 
Login with the 
credentials:  
- login: admin 
- password: switch 
 
 
- Delete the OmniSwitch 6360 configuration file: 
 
-> rm /flash/working/vcboot.cfg 
 
- Reboot the OmniSwitch: 
 
-> reload from working no rollback-timeout 
 
- It takes approximately 5 minutes for the OmniSwitch 6360 to reboot.

2 
PoE, VLAN & DHCP Configuration 
 
 1 
Introduction 
In this exercise, we will:  
- Check that the PoE is active on the OmniSwitch 6360.  
- Create several VLANs on the OmniSwitch 6360. We will use them in the next labs.  
- Once the PoE enabled and the VLANs created, we will configure the access point to get an IP address 
dynamically (DHCP). Reminder: In a previous lab, you have manually configured the IP address of the 
access point to 192.168.1.3.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
Notes 
In this lab, we will use the CLI to configure the PoE & VLAN features.   
It is interesting to note that this lab can also be done from the WebView (OmniSwitch administration web 
page). You can, therefore, if you wish, perform this whole lab from the WebView!  
 2 
Monitor the PoE on the OmniSwitch 6360 
The PoE (Power over Ethernet) is enabled by default on the OmniSwitch 6360. No configuration is needed. 
 
 
- Check the PoE settings on the OmniSwitch 6360 
- Monitor the PoE activity on the port 1/1/6 where is connected the OmniAccess 
Stellar AP1321 
 
To check that the PoE is enabled via CLI commands, double click on the OS-6360 shortcut available on the 
Windows desktop, then type the following commands:  
 
-> login: admin  
-> password: switch 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show lanpower slot 1/1 
 
Port Maximum(mW) Actual Used(mW)   Status    Priority   On/Off   Class   Type 
----+-----------+---------------+-----------+---------+--------+-------+---------- 
  //truncated 
  6     30000         7000       Powered On     Low      ON      4 ,- 
  7     30000            0       Searching      Low      ON      - ,-

<<<PAGE 123>>>
3 
PoE, VLAN & DHCP Configuration 
 
 3 
Creating VLANs on the OmniSwitch 6360 
3.1. 
Creating the VLANs  
 
Create the following VLANs:  
- VLAN 10 dedicated to the administration of the access point. 
- VLAN 20 dedicated to Employees. 
- VLAN 30 dedicated to Guests. 
Assign these VLANs to the ports 1/1/3 and 1/1/6. 
 
3.1.1. 
Creating the VLANs via the CLI 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 10 name Management-AP 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 20 name Employees 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 30 name Guests 
 
- Check the result:  
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show vlan 
vlan    type   admin   oper    ip    mtu          name 
------+-------+-------+------+------+------+------------------ 
[…] 
10     std       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    Management-AP 
20     std       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    Employees 
30     std       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    Guests 
[…] 
3.1.2. 
Assign the VLANs to the ports 
- Assign the VLANs to the port 1/1/3 (where the backbone is connected):  
The VLAN 10, 20 and 30 as tagged VLANs. 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 10 members port 1/1/3 tagged 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 20 members port 1/1/3 tagged 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 30 members port 1/1/3 tagged 
 
- Assign the VLANs to the port 1/1/6 (where is connected the access point):  
The VLAN 10 (Administration AP) as default/untagged VLAN.  
The VLAN 20 and 30 as tagged VLANs. 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 10 members port 1/1/6 untagged 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 20 members port 1/1/6 tagged 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 30 members port 1/1/6 tagged 
 
- Check the result: 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show vlan members port 1/1/3 
 
  vlan      type        status 
--------+-----------+--------------- 
     1    untagged    inactive 
    10    tagged      inactive 
    20    tagged      inactive 
    30    tagged      inactive 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show vlan members port 1/1/6 
 
  vlan      type        status 
--------+-----------+--------------- 
    10    untagged    forwarding 
    20    tagged      forwarding 
    30    tagged      forwarding

<<<PAGE 124>>>

 4 
Configure the Access Point in DHCP Mode 
Now that the VLAN 10 is assigned as the default VLAN on port 1/1/6 (where the access point is connected), it 
can join the DHCP server (located in the "backbone" area in the diagram below) and be assigned an IP address 
automatically.  
 
 
 
That's what we're going to do in this part.  
4.1. 
Enabling the uplink port on the OmniSwitch 6360  
Enable the port 1/1/3 (to connect the switch to the rest of the network > DHCP, …): 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> interfaces 1/1/3 admin-state enable

Conclusion 
In this lab, we have:  
- Verified that PoE was enabled, and that the access point was powered by PoE.  
- Created VLANs, which we will use in the next exercises.  
- Assigned these VLANs to different ports.   
- Once the VLANs were assigned to the ports, we have configured the access point to retrieve an IP address 
dynamically (DHCP).

Being connected on the OmniSwitch 6360 CLI interface, it can be useful to check the MAC addresses of the

To check the Stellar AP1321 MAC address from its web interface: 
 
Stellar AP1321 Web Interface   
From the web 
interface 
(192.168.1.3:8080 
or mywifi.al-
enterprise.com),

In the Detailed 
Information part, 
find and memorize 
the AP1321 MAC 
address

-> show mac-learning 
Legend: Mac Address: * = address not valid, 
 
        Mac Address: & = duplicate static address,

Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
 1 
Introduction 
In this lab, we will create:  
- A SSID « EmployeesX » (X = Stellan POD Number) dedicated to the company employees. 
- A SSID « GuestsX » (X = Stellan POD Number) dedicated to the guests/visitors. 
 
Once connected to the network, the guests/visitors must have different and restricted access rights than 
employees; therefore, they must be moved to a dedicated VLAN (that we have created in a previous 
exercise).  
 
All this configuration will be performed on the Stellar AP1321 and the OmniSwitch 6360.   
 
 
 
 2 
Creating the Employees SSID 
2.1. 
Connect to the Stellar AP1321  
 
Raspberry Pi « WifiClientX »  
Enter mywifi.al-
enterprise.com:8080 
in the URL bar 
 
Press Enter 
 
Select Administrator 
from the drop-down 
menu 
 
Password: superuser 
 
Click on Login 
 
 
 
2.2. 
Creating the Employees SSID

<<<PAGE 147>>>
4 
Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
 
- Create the « Employees » SSID 
 
Stellar AP1321 Web Interface  
Click on New in the WLAN window 
 
In the Create a new WLAN window, 
 
- WLAN name: EmployeesX (X = 
Stellan POD number) 
- Security: Personal 
- Password: superuser 
 
Leave the other parameters to their 
default values 
 
Click on Advanced 
 
  
 
Enter VLAN ID: 20 
 
Click on Save 
 
2.2.1. 
Verification 
 
 
Check that the « Employees » SSID has been created correctly 
 
Result 
The Employees SSID (EmployeesX) is created. It appears on the WLAN section: 
 
 
 
 
 
Employees Authentication

part in the appendix for more information)

R-Lab Windows Remote Desktop

 icon

(X = Stellan POD number)

if it is not displayed.

Enter the pre-shared key: 
superuser 
 
 
 
A Notification informs you 
that the client is connect to 
the SSID

(range > 192.168.20.70 to 192.168.20.79)

Creating the Guests SSID 
In the previous part, an « EmployeesX » SSID, dedicated to the employees, has been created. Let’s now see 
how to create an SSID dedicated to guests/visitors and discover the additional options available.  
3.1. 
Creating the Guests SSID  
 
Stellar AP1321 Web Interface 
Click on the New button in 
the WLAN section (previous 
tab) 
 
In the window Create a new 
WLAN, 
 
- WLAN name: GuestsX (X = 
Stellan POD number) 
- Security: Open 
- Captive Portal: Yes 
 
Click on Advanced 
 
Fill the VLAN ID: 30 
 
Click on Save 
 
Result 
The Guests SSID (GuestsX) is created. It appears on the WLAN section:

<<<PAGE 151>>>
8 
Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
3.2. 
Managing the Captive Portal 
 
 
- Configure the Captive Portal settings 
- Create the guest account « Guest » 
 
The internal captive portal is activated by default. Now the authentication type must be selected 
(account, access code, or terms of use):  
 
OmniAccess Stellar AP1321 Web Interface    
Open the Access menu 
 
Click on Authentication  
 
 
 
Select the authentication mode 
(here: Account) 
 
- If the authentication mode account 
is selected, an account must be 
created 
- If the authentication mode access 
code is selected, an access code must 
be created  
- If the authentication mode Terms of 
use is selected, the guest access 
attempt will be authorized if he 
accepts a terms of use (customizable) 
 
Click on Add and fill the information 
UserName: Guest 
Password: superuser 
 
The username and password fields 
are case sensitive (ex. The username 
“Guest” is different than “guest”) 
 
Fill the Starting Date and Ending Date 
(validity period account). 
 
Finally, click on Save 
 
  
 
 
 
3.3. 
Connecting to the Guests SSIDX 
 
 
Test the connection to the SSID « GuestsX » then check the settings 
 
WifiClientX  
Left-click on the 
 icon 
(top right) 
 
Select the SSID GuestsX (X = 
Stellar Number) 
Check under “More Networks” 
if it is not displayed. 
 
Click on Connect

on the network. He/She can 
access the network (ex.

to the network Wi-Fi card

192.168.30.79). 
The default gateway 192.68.30.7, which is the router’s IP interface address of sw7 for for 
the VLAN 30. 
 
 
Tips 
If you have time, you can test the other authentication modes of the captive portal (Account/Access 
Code/Terms of use).  
 4 
Conclusion 
In this exercise, we have created 2 SSID:  
- « EmployeesX » dedicated to the employees, and protected with a password 
- « GuestsX » dedicated to the guests, with an authentication via a Captive Portal (embedded in the 
Stellar AP)

Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
-ANNEXES- 
 5 
ANNEX: Configuring the AP as DHCP Server 
5.1. 
Configuring the Employees SSID Network Settings 
 
 
Configure the network settings to make the employees be automatically moved to a 
dedicated subnetwork.  
 
Once the Employees SSID created, the IP settings must now be configured. The aim is to automatically put 
the employee who connects to the « EmployeesX » SSID in a dedicated subnetwork. For employees, we 
will use the 192.168.10.0/24 subnetwork. 
 
Stellar AP1321 Web Browser 
Click on AP in the “AP” window 
 
 
Then, click on the AP IP 
address 
 
 
A new tab opens in the web 
browser  
 
Click on the Network menu 
then AP Networks 
Click on the Manage 
 
button in the vlan10 line 
 
Fill the following IP settings for 
the vlan10:  
- IP Address: 192.168.10.3 
- Subnetwork: 255.255.255.0 
- DNS: 192.168.10.3 
 
Click on Save

<<<PAGE 155>>>
12 
Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
5.2. 
Configuring the Internal DHCP Server 
 
 
- Activate the internal DHCP server on the OmniAccess Stellar AP1321 
- Create a range dedicated to employees 
 
To allow the employees who connect to the EmployeesX SSID to obtain an IP address, we are going to 
activate the DHCP server embedded on the AP1321:  
 
Stellar AP1321 Web Administration Interface   
Still in the recently opened tab 
 
Click on the Service menu 
 
Click on Create in the DHCP 
tab 
Fill the following settings in 
the new DHCP range:  
- Pool name: Employees 
- Subnet: 255.255.255.0 
- Gateway: 192.168.10.3 
- Range Start: 192.168.10.10 
- Range Stop: 192.168.10.50 
- DNS1: 192.168.10.3  
- Leave the other settings to 
their default values 
 
Click on Save 
 
 
 
Notes 
The DHCP range contains 40 IP addresses. So only 40 devices can be connected simultaneously. If a larger 
amount of employee devices is planned, it is of course possible to increase this amount.  
 
The DHCP range dedicated to employees is now created. It is then necessary to assign it to the network 
“vlan10”. This means that an employee in the VLAN 10 (Employees VLAN) will get its IP address in this 
range. 
 
Select Action in the drop-
down menu 
 
Select Bind Network

<<<PAGE 156>>>
13 
Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
In the window Bind 
Configuration  
 
Select Bind Network: vlan10  
 
Click on Save 
 
5.3. 
Configuring the Guests SSID Network Settings 
 
 
Configure the Guests SSID network settings 
 
Once the Guests SSID is created and the Captive Portal configured, the network settings must now be 
defined. The objective is to place the guest who connects to the « GuestsX » in a dedicated subnetwork. 
For the guests, we will use the subnetwork 192.168.20.0/24. 
 
OmniAccess Stellar AP1321 Web Interface    
Click on AP in the “AP” window 
 
 
Then click on the AP IP address 
 
A new tab opens in the web 
browser 
 
Click on the Network menu 
then on AP Networks 
 
Click on the Manage 
 
button in the vlan20 button

<<<PAGE 157>>>
14 
Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
Fill the following IP settings for 
the vlan20:  
- IP Address: 192.168.20.3 
- Subnetwork: 255.255.255.0 
- DNS: 192.168.20.3 
 
Click on Save 
 
5.4. 
Configuring the Internal DHCP Server 
To make it easier for the guests to obtain an IP address who connects to the SSID “GuestsX”, we are going 
to enable the DHCP server embedded on the OmniAccess Stellar AP1321.  
 
Stellar AP1321 Web Interface  
Still in the new tab 
 
Click on the Service menu 
 
Click on Create in the DHCP 
tab 
 
 
Fill the following settings in 
the new DHCP range:  
- Pool name: Guests 
- Subnet: 255.255.255.0 
- Gateway: 192.168.20.3 
- Range Start: 192.168.20.10 
- Range Stop: 192.168.20.50 
- DNS1: 192.168.20.3  
- Leave the other settings to 
their default values  
 
Click on Save 
 
 
 
Notes 
The DHCP range contains 40 IP addresses. So only 40 devices can be connected simultaneously. If a larger 
amount of guest devices is planned, it is of course possible to increase this amount.  
 
The DHCP range dedicated to employees is now created. It is then necessary to assign it to the network 
“vlan20”. This means that an employee in the VLAN 20 (Guests VLAN) will get its IP address in this range.

<<<PAGE 158>>>
15 
Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
Select Action in the drop-
down menu 
 
Select Bind Network 
 
 
 
 
 
In the window Bind Network,  
 
Select Bind Network : vlan20  
 
Click on Save

ANNEX: Logging the Uses Behavior 
The users behavior (online/offline) can be logged and sent automatically to a TFTP, SFTP or Syslog server:  
 
OmniAccess Stellar AP1321 Web Interface    
Open the Access menu 
 
Click on Authentication 
 
 
At the bottom of the page, enable 
Client Behavior Tracking 
 
Then, select the desired Log To 
Server (ex. TFTP server) 
 
  
 
Fill the following information:  
- Server IP address 
- Port 
- Chemin distant (optional) 
- Cycle (the log files will be sent to 
the TFTP server periodically, following 
the value entered here)

2019-07-26 11:30:03/48:e2:44:69:41:0b//192.168.20.16/dc:08:56:01:5c:70/Visiteurs1/2019-07-26 11:30:02/1/open/ONLINE

password

license

ACCOUNT CREATION
Sub-addressing  
MyMail@MyCompany.com
In OVC 10.4.3, a unique account (linked to a mail address) can only be assigned to one MSP portal.
If a user want access to multiple MSP portals, he must use different mail addresses:
• A unique mail address per MSP portal (MSP1: User1@MyCompany.com, MSP2: User2@MyCompany.com)
• Or using the sub-addressing method for his email
Sub-addressing (MyMail+[subaddress]@MyCompany.com)
• Creation of new email addresses, derived from the user mail address
• Each new mail address used to create an account is seen as a unique user ID
• Activation links, upon account creation, are sent to the original mail address.
• Supported by SMTP server and all major vendors (Gmail, yahoo, outlook.com, Protonmail,… )

MIGRATION FROM OMNIVISTA CIRRUS 4

<<<PAGE 218>>>
MIGRATION FROM OMNIVISTA CIRRUS 4 TO OMNIVISTA
CIRRUS
OmniVista CIRRUS : 10.4.3
OmniVista CIRRUS 4: 4.9R1
As part of the following software releases of 
OmniVista CIRRUS 4 and 
Current situation
• In the current version, there are no tools for 
migrating from OVC4 to OVC.
1) Preparation of OmniVista CIRRUS  
• Manually setup the initial configuration for the Access Point (creation of AP Group, Provisioning 
Configuration, SSID, Access Policy, and other objects) to match the configuration for the Access Point 
in OmniVista CIRRUS 4. Check then the configuration between OVC4 and OVC.
2) Migration
• Remove all the network devices from the device catalog in OmniVista CIRRUS 4 Device Catalog.
• Add all your network devices in OmniVista CIRRUS. Manually, or import a XLXs or CSV template.
• Wait for the network device next Call Home and onboard in OmniVista CIRRUS :
• On Stellar Access Point Wait for the next Call Home (up to 30 minutes), or restart the AP.
• On OmniSwitch Wait for the next Call Home (30 minutes by default), or restart the cloud-agent process.

<<<PAGE 219>>>
REMOVING DEVICES IN OMNIVISTA CIRRUS 4
• The serial number of a network device cannot be declared in both OmniVista CIRRUS 4 and 
OmniVista CIRRUS. Make sure to remove all your equipment first:
1. Select all equipment
2. Delete all equipment

2 
Lab: Reinitialization of the Remote Lab 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Objectives 
 
In this training, the OmniSwitches and some network links are already partially configured.  
 
In order to achieve this setup in the remote lab environment, a couple of actions are required. 
The OmniSwitches pre-configuration will be loaded automatically with a script located in a folder, on the 
desktop. 
Once these configurations are loaded, you will have your working network composed of three OmniSwitches 
and two Access Points. 
 
 2 
Network Setup 
 
The network is composed of three OmniSwitch, 6870, 6360 and 2360 and two Stellar AP 1301 and 1321. The 
physical connections between them are shown in the diagram below. 
The VLANs and IP addresses have been allocated and the Client VLANs have been also assigned.

<<<PAGE 240>>>
3 
Lab: Reinitialization of the Remote Lab 
 
 3 
Devices Configuration 
3.1. 
Reset the OmniSwitches 
 
The script used to reset the OmniSwitches has two objectives: erase any existing configuration and load the 
required configuration for this training. 
 
- On the Remote Desktop Connection, go to the folder “OmniVista CIRRUS 10” located on the desktop and 
double-click on the script called “reset_PODX”. 
 
 
- The script will close all the console connection of the POD devices. It is sequentially executed on APs and 
then OmniSwitches. 
 
 
In the OmniSwitch console window, DO NOT press any key during the reset process. 
Pressing Enter during the OmniSwitch reboot phase will lead you to the Miniboot of the switch 
and interrupt the reboot cycle. 
 
- Wait a couple of minutes until the boot process of the OmniSwitches is finished. 
 
3.2. 
Configuration check 
- Open a console connection on the SW7-6760A, SW5-6360A, SW4-2360 from the desktop. 
- Use the login “admin” and the password “switch”. 
- Verify the LAN Switch connectivity: 
On the OmniSwitches 
-> ping 192.168.100.102  (Connection to the DHCP server)  
-> ping www.google.com (Connection to Internet) 
 
- Open a console connection on the AP1301 from the desktop. 
- Use the login “support” and password “aos2016” (default). 
- Check the LAN connectivity : 
On AP1301 
-> ssudo ping 192.168.100.102  (Connection to DHCP server)  
-> ssudo ping www.google.com (Connection to Internet) 
 
 
Your AP1321 cannot ping the network because the OmniSwitch (OS6360) on which it is 
connected is not fully configured yet. The configuration of the OS6360 will be done in 
the following labs. 
You have now completed this lab.

3.3. 
OmniSwitch activation 
- 
In the Device Catalog, scroll horizontally to find the column “Activation Status” (Use the 
expand/collapse button 
 if you can’t scroll): 
The activation Status of the OmniSwitch is “Waiting for validation” during a short period. OmniVista 
Cirrus automatically validates the device. 
The activation status of the switches is then “Waiting for first Contact”.  
OmniVista Cirrus is waiting for the device to connect to it. 
 
Network devices automatically contact the OmniVista Cirrus Server at regular “call home” intervals. For 
these devices, there is no need to manually start or restart the activation process. The devices will 
automatically contact the OmniVista Cirrus Server at the configured Call Home interval and complete the 
activation process. 
 
The Activation process is performed automatically on the OmniSwitch and Stellar Access Points. But the 
call home interval might take too much time. You can force the call home with one of the following 
methods of your choice:

<<<PAGE 251>>>
11 
Lab: Environment Creation and OmniSwitch OnBoarding 
 
 
 
1. Restart the activation process manually (recommended method): 
OS6870 & OS6360 
-> cloud-agent admin-state restart 
 
2. Or reboot the devices as follow: 
OS6870 & OS6360 
-> reload from working no rollback-timeout 
 
For both cases, the Device Activation Status should display “Connected to OV”, followed by “Provisioning” 
and "OV Managed", meaning the device is successfully registered and ready for full management with the 
OmniVista Cirrus Server. This process should take about 2 minutes. 
 
 
 
Check the cloud-agent status on either OmniSwitch, via CLI:

<<<PAGE 262>>>
ACTIVATION STATUS - DETAILS
Activation Status
Description
Waiting for first contact
Device has been validated. OV Cirrus is waiting for the device to connect to it. Once the user connects to the network, the device 
will be ready for limited (unlicensed device) or full management (licensed device).
Obtaining Certificate
Device has contacted the OmniVista Cirrus server, and the server is creating a digital certificate that is used in creating the secure 
VPN channel between your device and the OmniVista Cirrus server.
Certificate Previously Issued
A certificate was previously provided to this network device, but it has contacted OmniVista Cirrus without it.
Failed to get certificate
Device has contacted the OmniVista Cirrus Server, and the server attempted to create a certificate to achieve a more secure 
communication, but the attempt has failed.
Registered 
Device has successfully registered with OmniVista Cirrus and is ready for limited management.
Upgrade
Device software version will be or is being upgraded.
Upgrading
Device is in the process of upgrading its software.
Upgrade Failed
Device software version upgrade failed.
Assigned
Device has contacted OmniVista Cirrus and was found to be assigned to your account. The device is in the process of becoming 
fully managed.
VPN Configuring
Device is in the process of configuring a secure transport channel for connecting to OmniVista Cirrus.
Factory Reset required
The VPN profile was changed/updated. A Factory Reset is required on the device.
Connected to OV
Device has connected to OmniVista Cirrus over a secure transport channel and will proceed with provisioning, if required.
Provisioning
Device is in the process of receiving configuration information from the Provisioning application, before ready for full mgmt.
Provisioning Failed
Device was unable to process the provisioning configuration sent by the Provisioning application, or OmniVista Cirrus was unable to 
discover the device.
OV Managed
Device is ready for full management.
Unsupported Device Model
OmniVista Cirrus does not support the device.

<<<PAGE 278>>>
BLE BEACONING
BLE Beaconing ready for the AP1230, AP13xx, AP14xx, AP15xx series with a built-in BLE
 
• Stellar APs ready for Asset Tracking Solution
• Asset: people or equipment (wheel chair, medical devices, laptop,…)
• Reducing time to find assets: improves employees/customer satisfaction
• BLE Beacon is configured per AP Group
• Turned OFF by default
• Configurable parameters are
• Beaconing Mode : iBeacon per default
• Transmission Power
• Frequency/Emission Period
• UUID (Universal Unique Identifier) – ALE specific UUID for all ALE products
• Major and Minor values – used for greater accuracy than UUID alone
OAW-AP

<<<PAGE 279>>>
INTEGRATION WITH AEROSCOUT LOCATION ENGINE
AeroScout RTLS (Real Time Location Services) provides location services.
• i.g: Tracking of employees in the building at the plant
 
• AeroScout solution utilize standard WiFi (802.11) technologies as a 
communication infrastructure
• Customers use the Stellar AP to communicate with AeroScout tags and deliver 
information to the AeroScout Location Engine
• AeroScout LBS Architecture
• AeroScout Tags: Device generating 802.11 messages at a predefined interval
• Stellar APs: Delivers RSSI measurements of tags and WiFi clients to the AeroScout Engine
• AeroScout Engine Server (AES): Location Engine. Based on RSSI measurements (from the 
Stellar AP), determine position of the clients
• AeroScout Engine Manager (AEM): Configuration of the AES. Displays clients on the map, 
heatmaps, analytics, Geofencing alerts

the switch information i.e. system location, system name…) TLVs.

Lab: Stellar Access Point On-boarding and AP Group 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Objectives 
 
In the previous labs, we have onboarded two OmniSwitches, the OS6870 and OS6360.  
Now, we are going to onboard and provision two Stellar Access Points AP1301 and AP1321. 
1.1. 
POD Topology 
 
 
 
1.2. 
Access to OmniVista Cirrus  
Connect to the OmniVista Cirrus instance with the following URL: https://eu.manage.ovcirrus.com  
Login: pod##@ale-training.com , replace ## by your POD number [25-32] 
Password: Superuser01!

<<<PAGE 296>>>
4 
Lab: Stellar Access Point On-boarding and AP Group 
 
 2 
Configuration required on the OmniSwitch 6360 
The Management VLAN (10) is not created and configured on the OmniSwitch 6360. Without this VLAN, the 
Stellar AP1321 (connected to the 6360) won’t be able to request an IP from the DHCP server and contact then 
OmniVista Cirrus. 
 
We will create and configure this VLAN on the OS-6360A. 
2.1. 
Stellar Access Points Management VLAN: VLAN 10 
The Stellar Access Points communicates with the network through an untagged VLAN. 
The VLAN to be used here is the VLAN 10. 
2.2. 
Management VLAN 10 port assignment   
VLAN 10 needs to be untagged on the OmniSwitch 6360 port where the access point is connected: 1/1/6. 
And the VLAN 10 is tagged on the uplink port of the Access OmniSwitch 6360: 1/1/3. 
2.3. 
Configure the OS-6360A 
Open a console connection to the OS-6360A, login (credentials: admin / switch), and enter the following 
commands: 
 
OS6360 
Login: admin 
Password: switch 
 
-> vlan 10 name AP-MGMT 
-> vlan 10 members port 1/1/6 untagged 
-> vlan 10 members port 1/1/3 tagged

<<<PAGE 297>>>
5 
Lab: Stellar Access Point On-boarding and AP Group 
 
 3 
Configuration required for the Access Points 
3.1. 
Unique Access Point Group: “My-AP-Group” 
The two Stellar Access Points require the same configuration. Meaning that they’ll belong to the same AP 
Group. 
Both Access Points are assigned to the newly created AP Group “My-AP-Group”. 
3.2. 
Unique Provisioning Configuration: “My-Provisioning-Config”   
Although they don’t require yet a specific configuration, both access points will inherit the same provisioning 
configuration. 
This configuration profile “My-Provisioning-Config” can be modified later if needed. 
 4 
Stellar Access Point AP1321 and AP1301 Onboarding 
We will now onboard the two Stellar Access Points on OmniVista Cirrus. 
4.1. 
Add the AP1321 to OmniVista Cirrus 
4.1.1. 
Create the Device 
 
- To on-board and manage your Access Point 1321, you must first add it to the device catalog. 
- Go to INVENTORY > DEVICE CATALOG and click on the tab Device List. 
- Click on the button “+ Create Device” to bring up the Create Device page. 
 
 
- In the Device Family, select “Stellar AP”. 
 
- In the Serial number, enter the Serial number of the Stellar Access Point AP1321: 
- On the desktop, open a console on the AP1321, login (credentials: support / aos2016)  and enter:

4.3. 
Stellar Access Point activation 
 
- In the Device Catalog, tab Device List, scroll horizontally to find the column “Activation Status”.  
o 
The activation Status of the Access Point is “Waiting for validation” during a short period. 
OmniVista Cirrus automatically validates the device.

o 
OmniVista Cirrus is waiting for the device to connect to it. 
 
 
 
Notes 
In this screenshot, only Stellar Access Points are displayed. This is because 2 filters have been selected: 
- 
“AP” filter in the top left corner. All, AP and Switch can be selected. 
- 
“My Site” in the top right corner. In an organization with multiple sites, select the appropriate site. 
 
- Network devices automatically contact the OmniVista Cirrus Server at regular “call home” intervals. For 
these devices, there is no need to manually start or restart the activation process. The devices will 
automatically contact the OmniVista Cirrus Server at the configured Call Home interval and complete the 
activation process. 
 
- The Activation process is performed automatically on the Stellar Access Points. But the call home interval 
might take too much time. You can force the call home by rebooting the Access Point. Use the credentials 
“support / aos2016” to log in:

Troubleshooting the Level 2

SSID CREATION
▪
How to create a new SSID?
• Wireless → SSID
▪
SSID
• Wizard driven tool.
• Selection of Pre-defined Usage (Guest, Employee, 
BYOD,…).
• All the configuration is performed from the wizard.

<<<PAGE 310>>>
AUTHENTICATION SECURITY LEVEL - REMINDER
▪
Open + Captive Portal
• Cons: No Security
• Pros: Followed by Captive Portal, any type of device can be 
authenticated
▪
MAC authentication
• Cons: MAC can be spoofed, no traffic encryption
• Pros: Available for basic wireless devices (printers, 
scanners,…)
▪
WPA/WPA2/WPA3 Personal = Pre-Shared Key (PSK)
• Pros: Easy set up, strong keys can be difficult to hack
• Cons: But all keys can be hacked or stolen (key shared by all 
users)
▪
WPA/WPA2/WPA3 Enterprise = 802.1X
• Pros: Strongest security, ease of Management, scalability
• Cons: More configuration during initial setup (server, users)
Level of Trust
Authentication Method

2/ DEVICE SPECIFIC PSK (DSPSK)
▪
How it works:
•
Option Device Specific PSK is enabled
•
Device performs MAC authentication
•
In the Company property database, a specific PSK pass
phrase is assigned to the MAC address of the device
•
The user must use this Passphrase for his device
•
If Device Specific PSK =
•
Force Device Specific PSK: No global PSK configured on the 
SSID level, only a specific passphrase per device is used.
•
Prefer Device Specific PSK: Device must use the passphrase
returned from the MAC authentication. If not configured, 
user can use the global PSK passphrase, on the SSID level.
➢Each device have a different key

<<<PAGE 327>>>

is NOT supported
•

with « Prefer Device Specific
PSK »
•
Device Specific PSK: Enabled
•
Prefer Device Specific PSK
•
Force Device Specific PSK

<<<PAGE 328>>>
3/ SSID CONFIGURATION - PRIVATE GROUP PSK (PPSK)
▪
Configuration:
•
Option Device Specific PSK: disabled, or set to 
« Prefer »
•
Activate Private Group PSK and enter one/multiple 
entries:
•
A name to differenciate the entries
•
A unique passphrase
•
A pre-configured Access Role Profile
▪
How it works:
•
A user connects to the SSID with one of the passphrase
in PPSK entries. He is assigned to the ARP attached.
•
A user can still use the global PSK passphrase, and is
assigned the ARP attached to the SSID.
➢Multiple PSK – associated to different ARP – available for all users

<<<PAGE 329>>>
4/ DYNAMIC PRIVATE GROUP PSK
▪
How it works:
•
Option Device Specific PSK: set to 
« Prefer »
•
Activate Dynamic Group PSK
•
Create A Dynamic Private Group PSK 
Profile:
•
One or multiple PSK entries
•
Each entry is linked to a VLAN ID and ARP
•
A different VLAN ID can be used with the 
same ARP in different entried
➢
No need to create as many ARP as VLANs 
required.
•
A user uses one passphrase from the 
list, and is assigned to the 
corresponding VLAN ID and ARP.

Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Objectives 
Now that the devices in the Remote Lab have been discovered in OmniVista Cirrus, let’s create multiple SSIDs 
(employee, guest…). In this first lab, we will focus on how to create a secured Employee SSID. 
1.1. 
POD Topology 
 
 
Steps to create an Employee SSID in the Remote Lab: 
1. Create VLAN 20. This VLAN will service the SSID “EmployeesX” (X=R-Lab number). It will be tagged 
from the Access Points to the Access OmniSwitches (2360 and 6360), and over the link to the 
OmniSwitch 6870.  
2. Create the SSID and configure its options.

<<<PAGE 335>>>
4 
Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
 2 
Employee VLAN Configuration 
Before creating the Employee SSID, let’s create the VLAN that will be associated to this EmployeeX SSID (X= 
R-Lab number) and that will carry the employee traffic.  
 
 
Create VLAN 20 on the OmniSwitches 6870, 6360 and 2360. 
 
 
 
- The VLAN application of Omnivista Cirrus will be used to create and manage the Employee VLAN 
- Go to LAN > Layer 2 > VLAN.  
- Click on + Create VLAN. 
 
 
 
- In the Create VLAN page, configure the following: 
o 
VLAN IDs: 20 
o 
VLAN(s) Admin Status: Enabled 
o 
VLAN Description: Employee 
 
- In the Switch Selection section, select both the OS-6870A and OS-6360A 
 
- In the Selected Devices section, click on the Add Ports link in the Q Tagged Ports column for the OS6870-
A: 
o 
In the Port Selection window, choose ports 1/1/3 and 1/1/8 and move them to the right section 
of the window using the > button. Click Ok. 
 
- In the Selected Devices section, click on the Add Ports link in the Q Tagged Ports column for the OS6360-
A:

<<<PAGE 336>>>
5 
Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
o 
In the Port Selection window, choose ports 1/1/3 and 1/1/6 and move them to the right section 
of the window using the > button. Click Ok. 
 
 
 
- Click Create. 
- Employee VLAN 20 now appears in the VLANs list (use the refresh button if it is not): 
 
 
 
- As the OS2360 is not managed by Omnivista Cirrus, we are going to open a Console session to create the 
VLAN manually. Double-click on the OS-2360 console icon and type the following commands: 
 
 
 
vlan 20 name EMPLOYEE 
vlan 20 members port 1/1/6 tagged 
vlan 20 members port 1/1/8 tagged

<<<PAGE 337>>>

Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
 3 
Employee IP interface Configuration 
Now let’s create the IP interface associated to the Employee VLAN on the OS6870 
 
 
OS6360 and OS2360 will act as Layer 2 devices, therefore no IP interface is required on these switches.  
 
 
- Go to LAN > Layer 3 > IP Interface. 
- Click on + Create IP Interface 
- Configure the following in the IP Interface Information page. 
o 
Name: int_employees 
o 
IP Address: 192.168.20.7 
o 
Subnet Mask: 255.255.255.0 
o 
Device Type: VLAN 
o 
VLAN ID: 20 
o 
IP Forward: Enabled 
 
- In the Switch Selection section, select the OS-6870A 
- Click Create. 
- IP interface int_employees should now appear in the IP interface list. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Notes > SSH  
You could have configured the Employee VLAN using the SSH connection to the switches from Omnivista Cirrus. 
Also, you can perform monitoring commands to make sure that the configuration was applied correctly.

 4 
Employee SSID Creation 
Now that we have the Employee VLAN and the associated IP interface, let’s create the Employee SSID: 
 
 
4.1. 
Employee SSID Creation 
 
 
Create the SSID EmployeesX (X = R-Lab Number) 
 
> Select Wireless > SSIDs 
  > Click on the + Create SSID button 
 > In the Basic Information section complete the following: 
    > Profile Name: EmployeesX (X = R-Lab number) 
    > SSID: <filled automatically> 
    > Usage: Enterprise Network for Employees (802.1X) 
 
 
    
 
Notes > About the “Usage” 
During the SSID creation, a “Usage” is asked. When you select a Usage, relevant related default configurations 
(Access Policy, Authentication Strategy, …) are automatically created.  
Of course, these configurations can be customized. Check the Omnivista Cirrus dedicated Help for more 
information.  
 
  > Encryption Type: WPA2_AES  
  > Allowed Band: 2.4GHz and 5GHz 
   
 
Tips > Help Menu 
As you can see, several settings can be managed in the SSID Creation properties. Take the time to learn more 
about each of them by clicking on the Help button   
 
 
Notes > UPAMRadiusServer 
In this lab, for all the types of authentication, we will use the UPAM platform (Unified Policy Authentication 
Manager) embedded in OmniVista Cirrus.  
UPAM is a unified access management platform for both AOS Switch Series devices and Stellar AP Series 
devices. UPAM supports both captive portal server and RADIUS server; and can be used to implement multiple 
authentication methods, such as MAC authentication, 802.1X authentication, and captive portal authentication. 
 
> In the Authentication Strategy section complete the following:   
  > RADIUS Server: UPAMRadiusServer 
  > Click on Manage Employee Accounts 
  > Select Launch ‘Manage Employee Accounts’ page in a new browser tab

<<<PAGE 339>>>
8 
Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
4.2. 
Employee Account Creation 
 
 
Create the Employee account 
 
> Click on the + Create Employee Account button 
  > Username: Employee  
  > Password: password 
  > Repeat Password: password 
  > Do not modify other attributes 
  > Click on Create 
 
 
Tips > Importing Employee Accounts Information 
You can automatically import a csv/xlsx file containing Employee Account information 
by clicking on the Import button at the top of the screen.    
4.3. 
Back to… Creating the EmployeesX SSID 
 
Return to the Create SSID window 
In the Authentication Strategy > Access Policy section configure the following: 
  > Select Configure Access Policy 
  > Leave the Policy Name to its assigned name. 
  > Set the Authentication Source option to Local Database 
  > Set the Web Authentication option to None 
 
 
Web Authentication 
For Employee networks, typically a Captive Portal is not required as a different type of 
authentication is used (802.1x in this example).   
 
In the Default VLAN/Network section configure the following: 
  > Select Configure Access Role Attributes 
  > Leave the Profile Name to its assigned name  
 
- For now, we are leaving all the other additional SSID settings to their default values. 
- Click Next.

4.4. 
Assigning the SSID to the AP Group 
 
 
Assign the SSID EmployeesX to the AP Group created in the previous lab 
 
Now that the SSID EmployeesX has been created, it has to be assigned to one or multiple AP Group(s): 
 
In the Network Assignments section 
> Click on + Add Site button 
  > In the Select Site to filter groups option, select your Site “My Site” 
  > In the Select Access Points Groups option, select My-AP-Group 
  > Also, Remove default device group from the list 
> Click Next 
 
In the Schedule and VLAN mappings section 
> Click on Edit button that is in the VLAN/Tunnel Mapping column and on your “My-AP-Group” line. 
  > In the VLAN/Tunnel Mapping window, set the Choose Network Mapping to VLAN 
  > Set the VLAN ID to 20 
  > Click Apply 
> Click Create to complete the SSID creation. 
 
 
Setting a Schedule 
By default, the availability schedule for AP Groups is set to "Always Available". However, you can schedule 
availability for specific times/days of the week. You can set the same availability schedule for all selected AP 
Groups, or set different schedules for each group.

UPAM OVERVIEW

<<<PAGE 351>>>
UNIFIED POLICY AUTHENTICATION MANAGER - UPAM
▪
UPAM applications
• Guest Access (Guest Access Strategy and Accounts)
• BYOD Access (BYOD Access Strategy and Accounts)
▪
UPAM consists of
• Guest Access
• BYOD Access
• A built-in RADIUS Server
• A built-in MAC Authentication Server

<<<PAGE 352>>>
UPAM – GUEST AND BYOD ACCESS
Employee users access the corporate 
network with their personal devices
Authentication via a « BYOD » Captive 
Portal
Captive Portal and employee users 
managed in UPAM BYOD
BYOD
GUEST ACCESS
◼Guest users are granted limited access
to the corporate network
◼Authentication via « Guest » Captive 
Portal
◼Captive Portal and guests users
managed in UPAM Guest

<<<PAGE 353>>>
UPAM - SERVICES
• Authentication Server
• Internal RADIUS server used to authenticate both Guest and BYOD users
• E-mail server
• Guest sponsor approval
• External Log Server
• UPAM logs can be redirected to an external syslog server
• Guest Access Management
• Dedicated Captive Portal and database
• BYOD Access Management
• Dedicated Captive Portal and database

<<<PAGE 354>>>
UPAM – AUTHENTICATION STRATEGY
• Guests and Employees are authenticated by:
• Internal RADIUS Server (with Local Database)
• External RADIUS servers
• IMSI/IMEI Database (with Celona Edge)
• Cloud Identity (with Azure Active Directory)
• In Authentication Strategy, specify the authentication server that will be used

<<<PAGE 355>>>
UPAM – AUTHENTICATION STRATEGY
• Advanced Options
• Network Enforcement Policy
• Default Role and Policy List of the user if the 
Authentication server doesn’t return a role
• Other attributes
• Session timeout, Accounting Interim Interval, 
Upstream/Downstream bandwidth
• Web Redirection
• Web Authentication – which Captive Portal 
template is returned
• Guest/BYOD Access Strategy
• How the guest/BYOD users are managed (login 
strategy, self-registration,…)
• Creation of Guest/Employee Accounts

<<<PAGE 356>>>
UPAM
GUEST ACCESS STRATEGY

<<<PAGE 357>>>
UPAM – GUEST ACCESS STRATEGY
• Guest Access Strategy defines:
• Login Strategy
• How the Guest is authenticated: credentials, 
access code, Terms & conditions, Simple 
Persona (no T&C)

<<<PAGE 358>>>
UPAM – GUEST ACCESS STRATEGY
• Guest Access Strategy defines:
• Post Portal Enforcement
• Provide a new Role to the guest after the 
portal authentication.
• Self-registration strategy
• The Guest can create its own username & 
password
• An Employee can validate the guest account 
creation

Lab: Guest SSID Creation 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Objectives 
In the previous lab, we have learned how to create an Employee SSID dedicated for company employees. 
Now, let’s see how to create a Guest SSID dedicated for Guest users. 
1.1. 
POD Topology 
 
Steps to create a Guest SSID in the Remote Lab: 
1. Create VLAN 30. This VLAN will service the SSID “GuestsX” (X = R-Lab Number). It will be tagged from 
the Access Points to the Access OmniSwitches (2360 and 6360), and over the link to the OmniSwitch 
6870.  
2. Create the SSID and configure its options.

<<<PAGE 375>>>
4 
Lab: Guest SSID Creation 
 
 2 
Creation of the Guest VLAN & IP Interface 
Before creating the Guest SSID, let’s create the VLAN and IP interface that will be associated to this SSID 
GuestsX (X= R-Lab number) and that will carry the guest traffic.  
2.1. 
Guest VLAN 
 
Create the VLAN 30 on the OmniSwitches 6870, 6360 and 2360.

o 
VLAN IDs: 30 
o 
VLAN(s) Admin Status: Enabled 
o 
VLAN Description: Guest

- In the Selected Devices section below, click on the Add Ports link in the Q Tagged Ports column for the 
OS6870-A: 
o 
In the Port Selection window, choose ports 1/1/3 and 1/1/8 and move them to the right section 
of the window using the > button. Click Ok. 
- In the Selected Devices section, click on the Add Ports link in the Q Tagged Ports column for the OS6360-
A: 
o 
In the Port Selection window, choose ports 1/1/3 and 1/1/6 and move them to the right section 
of the window using the > button. Click Ok.

2.2. 
Guest IP interface 
Now let’s create the IP interface associated to the Guest VLAN on the OS6870 
 
OS6360 and OS2360 will act as Layer 2 devices, therefore no IP interface is required on these switches.  
 
- Go to LAN > Layer 3 > IP Interface. 
- Click on + Create IP Interface 
- Configure the following in the IP Interface Information page. 
o 
Name: int_guests 
o 
IP Address: 192.168.30.7  
 
o 
Subnet Mask: 255.255.255.0 
o 
Device Type: VLAN 
o 
VLAN ID: 30 
o 
IP Forward: Enabled 
 
- In the Switch Selection section, select the OS-6870 
- Click Create. 
- IP interface int_guests should now appear in the IP interface list.

Guest SSID Creation

Notes > About the “Usage” 
During the SSID creation, a “Usage” is asked. When you select a Usage, relevant related default configurations 
(Access Policy, Authentication Strategy, …) are automatically created.  
Of course, these configurations can be customized. Check the Omnivista Cirrus dedicated Help for more 
information.  
 
 
Tips > Help Menu 
As you can see, several settings can be managed in the SSID Creation properties. Take the time to learn more 
about each of them by clicking on the Help button   
 
 
Notes > UPAMRadiusServer 
In this lab, for all the types of authentication, we will use the UPAM platform (Unified Policy Authentication 
Manager) embedded in OmniVista Cirrus.  
UPAM is a unified access management platform for both AOS Switch Series devices and Stellar AP Series 
devices. UPAM supports both captive portal server and RADIUS server; and can be used to implement multiple 
authentication methods, such as MAC authentication, 802.1X authentication, and captive portal authentication. 
 
> In the Authentication Strategy section complete the following:   
  > RADIUS Server: UPAMRadiusServer 
  > Click on Manage Guest Accounts 
  > Select Launch ‘Manage Guest Accounts’ page in a new browser tab

In the Authentication Strategy > Access Policy section configure the following:

Web Authentication 
For Guest networks, a Captive Portal is required to authenticate the guest users.   
 
  > Click on Create Guest Access Strategy

  > Layout Information: Select any of the available layout types

  > Return to the Guest Access Strategy window

  > In the Login Strategy section select:

UPAM Captive Portal will be used   
 
  > Return to the Create SSID window

Setting a Schedule 
By default, the availability schedule for AP Groups is set to "Always Available". However, you can schedule 
availability for specific times/days of the week. You can set the same availability schedule for all selected AP

Client session 
You can find additional information and details about the client who just got connected to the SSID: 
- Go to Network > Analytics > Clients. 
 
A list of clients is split into 4 categories: the live wired and wireless clients, and the session history of the 
wired and wireless clients.

history.

network events, RSSI, Client throughput, PHY rates,…

Lab: Creation of an Employee SSID for BYOD 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Objectives 
In the previous lab, we have learned how to create secured Employees and Guests SSIDs. Now, let’s see how 
to create an Employee BYOD SSID, dedicated for the employees who want to bring and use their personal 
device within the company network. 
 
1.1. 
POD Topology 
 
 
Steps to create the BYOD Employee SSID in the remote lab: 
- For this SSID, no additional VLANs need to be created: we will reuse the VLAN 20 (Employee) and 30 
(Guest). The BYOD employee device will be placed first in the Guest VLAN (pre-authentication). Once 
authenticated via a Captive Portal, it will be moved to the Employee VLAN(post-authentication). 
- Create the SSID and configure its options.

<<<PAGE 392>>>
4 
Lab: Creation of an Employee SSID for BYOD 
 
 2 
Creation of the BYOD SSID 
Let’s create the “BYODX” SSID: 
 
2.1. 
Creating the “BYODX” SSID: 
 
Create the SSID “BYODX” (X = R-Lab Number) 
 
> Select Wireless > SSIDs 
  > Click on the + Create SSID button 
> In the Basic Information section, complete the following: 
    > Profile Name: BYODX (X = R-Lab number) 
    > SSID: <filled automatically> 
    > Usage: Employee BYOD Network  
     > Enable Band: 2.4 GHz and 5 GHz 
    > Enhanced Open: Disabled 
 
 
Notes > About the “Usage” 
During the SSID creation, a “Usage” is asked. When you select a Usage, a relevant related default configuration 
(Access Policy, Authentication Strategy, …) is automatically created.  
Employee BYOD Network > create a network for employees connecting with their own devices. Suitable for 
setting up an Open Network for Employee BYOD devices. Access to the network is granted after BYOD portal 
authentication. 
 
 
Tips > Help Menu 
As you can see, several settings can be managed in the SSID Creation properties. Take the time to learn more 
about each of them by clicking on the Help button.  
 
> In the section Authentication Strategy: 
  > In RADIUS Server, keep UPAMRadiusServer 
  > Select the option Configure Access Policy. This action displays new parameters bellow. 
  > Keep the Authentication Source to None 
  > Keep the Web Authentication with Employee. The BYOD Captive Portal is displayed with this option. 
   > For BYOD ACCESS Strategy, click on Create BYOD Access Strategy > Launch ‘Create BYOD Access 
Strategy’ page in a new browser tab.   
 
- Configure a new BYOD Access Strategy in the new tab: 
> In the page BYOD Access Strategy: 
  > Set the name: My_BYOD_Strategy 
  > Select the option Captive Portal template: My_CPTemplate_X.  
      > We are using the same template (look and feel) than the Guest Captive Portal. 
  > Select Authentication Source: Local Database 
      > The Employee user is known in the internal Database of OVNG (Employee / password). 
 
> In the section Post Portal Authentication Enforcement:

<<<PAGE 393>>>
5 
Lab: Creation of an Employee SSID for BYOD 
 
  > Access Role Profile: click on Create Access Role Profile > Launch ‘Create Access Role Profile’ page 
in a new browser tab. 
 
- Configure a new Access Role Profile in the new tab. It will be assigned as the final Role, once the client 
has been authenticated with the Captive Portal: 
> In the section Access Role Profile Settings: 
  > Set the name: “Employee_BYODX”, (Replace X with Rlab number) 
  > Click on Next  
> In the section Network Assignments: 
  > Select Group Assignment, and then select My-AP-Group 
  > Click on Next 
> In the section VLAN mappings: 
  > Click on the Edit button, on the My-AP-Group line. 
  > Select Choose Network Mapping: VLAN 
  > Enter VLAN: 20, (delete any other VLAN values than 20) 
      > The VLAN 20 is the Employee VLAN created previously on our Network devices.  
  > Click on Apply 
  > Click on Create 
 
- Go back to the tab “BYOD Access Strategy”: 
> In the section Post Portal Authentication Enforcement: 
  > Set Access Role Profile: “Employee_BYODX”, (Replace X with Rlab number) 
  > Click on Create  
 
- Go back to the tab “Create SSID”: 
> In the section Authentication Strategy: 
  > Set the BYOD Access Strategy: “My_BYOD_Strategy” 
> In the section Default VLAN/Network: 
  > Select Configure Access Role Attributes 
  > Keep the default Profile Name 
> Click on Next 
 
- Now that the SSID BYODX has been created, it must be assigned to one or multiple AP Group(s): 
In the section Network Assignment: 
> Click on + Add Site button 
  > In the Select Site to filter groups option, select your Site “My Site” 
  > In the Select Access Points Groups option, select “My-AP-Group” 
  > Also, Remove default device group from the list 
> Click Next 
 
- The last part of the SSID creation consists of assigning the Guest VLAN to the AP Group. The Employee 
will be pushed to the Employee VLAN once he will be authenticated, with the Post Portal Enforcement 
configuration: 
In the section Schedule and VLAN mappings: 
> Click on Edit button that is in the VLAN/Tunnel Mapping column, and on the My-AP-Group line. 
  > In the VLAN/Tunnel Mapping window, set the Choose Network Mapping to VLAN 
  > Set the VLAN ID to 30 (Guest VLAN) 
  > Click Apply 
> Click Create to complete the SSID creation. 
 
 
 
 
Setting a Schedule 
By default, the availability schedule for AP Groups is set to "Always Available". However, you can schedule 
availability for specific times/days of the week. You can set the same availability schedule for all selected AP 
Groups or set different schedules for each group. 
 
Now that we have finished the configuration of the SSID, let’s test it!

 4 
Monitoring the Connections 
 
Display the BYODX authentication record  
4.1. 
UPAM Monitoring 
4.1.1. 
Authentication Record 
The UPAM platform (Unified Policy Authentication Manager) is embedded in the OmniVista Cirrus. This 
module is used to implement authentication (MAC authentication, 802.1x, Captive Portal…) 
 
The Authentication Record Screen displays authentication information for all devices authenticated 
through UPAM:  
 
> Select Network > Access Records > Authentication Records 
 
 
 
In the Authentication Record Additional information, under the Authentication section, you can find the 
VLAN as well as the Access Role Profile: Employee_BYODX.  
You can see that this record informs you about the final state of the client, because the ARP 
Employee_BYODX is assigned after a successful Captive Portal authentication (post Portal enforcement). 
 
4.1.2. 
Captive Portal Access Record 
To monitor the Captive Portal access: 
 
> Select Network > Access Records > Captive Portal Records

<<<PAGE 397>>>
9 
Lab: Creation of an Employee SSID for BYOD 
 
You can find two entries: one with the account name “Guest”, which was use during the Guest lab. And 
one with the “Employee” account, used during this BYOD lab. 
In the additional Information page, confirm that you are using the expected account name, portal type, 
and the authentication is successful.  
 
4.2. 
Client session 
You can find additional information and details about the client who just got connected to the SSID: 
- Go to Network > Analytics > Clients. 
- Select the first tab ”Live Wireless Clients”, where our client was just authenticated.  
- Click on the Client MAC of your Wireless client. This leads you to a new page, the client session 
history. 
- Take a moment to analyse the different data gathered for this client: roaming timeline, alert entries, 
network events, RSSI, Client throughput, PHY rates,…

UNIFIED POLICIES & POLICIES LIST

<<<PAGE 404>>>
POLICY LIST
• Policy List
• List of Policy Rules (QoS, ACLs) 
• Action can be
• Accept/drop
• Bandwidth control
• Priority, 802.1p, DSCP marking
• Enforcement is bidirectional
• Policy List Assignment
• From RADIUS (account)
• From Access Role Profile (Default Policy List)
• Rule: "http-traffic"
✓Action: Accept
• Rule: "Network-traffic"
✓Action: Deny
• Rule: "Guest-speed"
✓Action: 1Mb/s
• Rule: "Guest-priority"
✓Action: 802.1p=3
Policy List:
"Policy-Guest"
Access Role 
Profile

<<<PAGE 405>>>
POLICY LIST - CONFIGURATION
• Unified Policy configuration
• From the application Unified Access – Unified Policies
• Configure the policies first
• Policy List configuration
• From the application Unified Access – Unified Policies List
• Assign the policies in the policy list
• Assign the Policy List to an Access Role Profile

<<<PAGE 406>>>
LOCATION AND PERIOD POLICIES
• A Location Policy defines a specific location where a device can access the network
• A Period Policy specifies the days and times during which a device can access the network
• Both policies are applied to devices classified into the Access Role Profile.

<<<PAGE 407>>>
BANDWIDTH CONTROL

<<<PAGE 408>>>
BANDWIDTH CONTROL
• Bandwidth contract at SSID level
• Configured in “Detailed SSID Settings”
• Bandwidth assigned per SSID and per AP,
shared between all users connected to the SSID
• Bandwidth contract at Access Role Profile level
• Configured in “Bandwidth Control” section
• Bandwidth assigned to the users using this profile
• Bandwidth contract at Role level
• A Policy List (ACL/QoS) can restrict 
the Bandwidth as an action
• Bandwidth limited by the ACL/QoS Rule

Lab: Unified Policies 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Objectives 
Now that the devices can connect to either Employe or Guest SSID. Let’s create Unified policies to control the 
access these users have to the network. 
1.1. 
POD Topology 
 
 
Steps to create a Unified Policy in the Remote Lab: 
1. Configure a Unified Policy in the Network Access Section.  
2. Assign the Unified Policy to the existing SSID.

<<<PAGE 413>>>
3 
Lab: Unified Policies 
 
 2 
Creation of the Unified Policy 
 
For this lab, we are going to make the test using the Employee SSID. Make sure the 
client is connected to the Employee SSID before proceeding.  
Still connected to the EmployeesX SSID, open the virtual client and ping the IP address assigned to VLAN 20 
on the OS6870 (192.168.20.7). Also open a ssh connection to the switch using this same IP address with the 
command ‘ssh admin@192.168.20.7’. If you are asked to validate the ssh connection, enter “yes”. The 
password is ‘switch’ 
Both should be successful.  
 
  
 
We have validated that we can reach the OS6870 in SSH. 
The first step is to define the type of policy that will be created. In this example, a Unified Policy that blocks 
ssh access to the network device OS6870 will be created.  
2.1. 
Unified Policy 
 
 
Create the Unified Policy in Omnivista Cirrus 
 
 
- Go to Network Access > Unified Access > Unified Policies 
- Click the + Create Unified Policy button. 
 
The first step is to create a Policy Condition. 
 
A policy Condition defines the type of traffic that will be inspected in the network devices.

<<<PAGE 414>>>
4 
Lab: Unified Policies 
 
 
   > In the Basic Information section configure the following: 
      > Policy Name: Block_SSH 
 
   > In the Condition section configure the following: 
      > Click the + Add Condition button 
   > In the Choose a Condition box, select L3 IPs 
   > In the Destination IP Address Range configure the following: 
      > Select Subnet Mask 
      > IP Address: 192.168.20.0  
 
      > Subnet Mask: 255.255.255.0 
 
   > Click on the + Add Another Condition button 
   > In the Choose a Condition box, select L4 Services 
      > L4 Service Conditions: Port(s) 
      > Service Protocol : TCP 
      > In the Destination Port box, we want to select SSH(22), but it does not exist. 
      > In front of Destination Port, click on the button Add New > Launch ‘Create Service Port’ page in a 
new browser tab. 
 
- In the new tab, we will create the SSH service port  
   > Name the service Port: SSH 
   > In the Protocol & Port section: 
      > Protocol : TCP 
      > Port Number : 22 
   > Click on Create 
 
- Back to the Unified Policy tab, we will complete the Policy creation  
   > In front of Destination Port, select SSH (22) 
 
- The next step is to define the Action  
 
A policy action defines the treatment that will be given to the traffic that matches the condition.  
 
   > In the QoS Action section configure the following: 
   > Accessibility: Drop 
 
 
Tips > Policy Actions 
You can see the different actions that can be applied to the traffic such as: L2 and L3 priority, and BW 
assignment with Max Output Rate and Three Color Marker (TCM) parameters.  
 
- Click Next. 
- Define the network devices that will be using this policy 
 
   > Select Group Assignment 
   > Select “My-AP-Group” 
   > Make sure default device group is not selected. 
 
 
Tips > All Sites 
You can filter the AP Groups from different sites using the All Sites by selecting a specific Site.  
- Click Create.

<<<PAGE 415>>>
5 
Lab: Unified Policies 
 
 3 
Assign the Unified Policy to the SSID 
  
Once the Unified Policy is created, then it must be assigned to the Access Role Profile that is associated to 
the SSID in which the Policy will be applied. 
 
- Go to Wireless > SSID. 
- Select the EmployeesX SSID from the SSID List and click Edit. 
 
 
- In the Customize SSID window, scroll down to the Default VLAN/Network section 
- In the ACL/QoS box, select the Block_SSH policy that was created in the previous step. 
 
 
 
Tips > Unified Policies 
In this step, multiple Unified Policies could be assigned to the Access Role Profile depending on the network 
access requirements.  
 
 
 
 
 
Tips > Advanced QoS Settings 
If you select the More Settings for Default VLAN / Network option on this step, you can see the advanced QoS 
options that can be configured such as Location Policy, Period (Time-Based) Policy, Bandwidth Control, and 
more.  
 
 
- Click Next 
- As we are not modifying any other parameter, click Next and Save in the Steps 2 and 3 windows.

<<<PAGE 416>>>
6 
Lab: Unified Policies 
 
 4 
Testing the Unified Policy 
 
Test the Unified Policy applied to the EmployeesX SSID 
 
As it was mentioned in the initial section of this lab, this test is applied when the client is connected to 
the EmployeesX SSID. Make sure your client is still connected to this SSID. 
 
At the beginning of the lab we tested that pinging the IP address assigned to VLAN 20 on the OS6870 
(192.168.20.7) and the ssh connection to the switch using this same IP address with the command ‘ssh 
admin@192.168.20.7’ are successful when the user is connected to the EmployeesX SSID 
 
 
Repeat these tests but now that the Block_SSH unified policy is assigned to the Access Role Profile 
associated to the EmployeesX SSID we will see a different result. 
 
  
 
You can see that pinging the switch is still successful but the ssh connection is now denied due to the QoS 
Unified Policy.

DISTRIBUTED RADIO MANAGEMENT - DRM
• Fully distributed control Plane

• RF context sharing

AP, power…

• Limited to neighbor APs
• Does not rely on AP Group or AP management 
vlan

• RF Profile applied to AP Group or at AP level
• Country Code set in the RF Profile

SCANNING
• Background scanning
• Each radio periodically scans the air – One channel at the time 
• During scanning wireless clients are impacted – no 802.11 data
• Scanning is required for WIPS
• Interfering & Rogue AP detection / Wireless attack detection 
• Scanning Interval and duration
• Default interval = 20 sec – Range = 5-10800 sec  
• Default Duration = 50 ms – Range = 50-110 ms
• Dedicated AP scanning mode
• AP only used to scan the air in order to check the quality of the 
wireless environment
• Voice and Video Awareness 
• Bypass scanning when the AP has an active voice or video session 
from a client
• SIP and H.323 traffic detected
